### UI compiler

| # | Task | Notes |
|---|------|-------|
| 215 | An unsupported UI `layout` primitive (any of them, not just spec-aspirational ones) vanishes with **zero trace** in the default Htmx codegen target — not even the diagnostic comment meant to keep the gap visible | `crates/ui/src/layout.rs`'s own doc comment states "any unrecognised call emits an HTML comment so the file stays valid" — true for the legacy `--html` mode, but in `crates/ui/src/server.rs:325-328` (the default Htmx path `certo-ui.exe` uses without `--html`, the one item 88's own writeup emphasizes is what real projects use), the generated handler explicitly filters out every line starting with `<!--` from `layout_to_html`'s output before splicing it into the page body. Confirmed via repro: a view using unrecognized layout primitives compiles clean and generates a handler body with the unsupported content silently absent — no comment, no warning, nothing. Small, bounded, standalone fix — the filter itself needs to stop stripping diagnostic comments (or route them somewhere visible, e.g. a build-time warning), independent of whether spec §10.2's own vocabulary gap below is ever built. |
| 216 | Spec §10.2's entire "dashboard" view vocabulary — `Column`/`Row`/`MetricCard`/`Table(...) { column(...) action(...) }` — doesn't exist, and the `layout: <expr>` colon syntax the same section's examples use doesn't parse either (`layout = <expr>` is the real, working form) | `crates/ui/src/layout.rs` only recognizes `VStack`/`HStack`/`Text`/`Heading`/`Link`/`Button`/`Image`/`Input`/`For`/`If` — confirmed via source read and repro. This is a full metric-card/data-table dashboard DSL, distinct from the currently-implemented simple CRUD list/form vocabulary (items 87/153/166) — a genuinely new, real feature, not a small tweak. Design-sketch-needed, matching the scale of item 87's own precedent for `@ui.generate`; the `layout:`-vs-`layout=` mismatch alone is a trivial spec-text fix and doesn't need to wait on the vocabulary question. |
| 89 | React/PWA/Flutter UI targets | Htmx is the only target; no plans to add the others without a specific customer need. |
| 183 | `List.of(...T)` and variadic functions in general remain unbuilt — **re-examined, downgraded from "low-urgency gap" to "declined pending an actual use case"** | Looked again at this item's real scope rather than just re-confirming the gap exists. `List.of`'s own spec table row (`fn<T>(...T): List<T>`) is the *only* place in either spec document that uses or describes variadic-parameter syntax anywhere — no other stdlib function, no user-facing example, nothing (checked via direct grep for `...T`/`...args`/`variadic`/rest-parameter patterns across both spec docs, not just re-asserted from memory). And the thing `List.of(1, 2, 3)` would provide is already fully available today: `[1, 2, 3]` list-literal syntax, confirmed by a real compiled program that method-chains directly off a literal (`[1, 2, 3].map(double)`) with no intermediate `val` needed — so there is no known gap in what a Certo program can actually express, only a spec line describing an alternate spelling for something list literals already cover. Building real variadic-function support (parser grammar, arity-flexible call-site typing, HIR/MIR lowering to a runtime array/list construction — genuinely a new, multi-crate language feature, not bounded stdlib work) purely to satisfy one redundant constructor is disproportionate to the actual benefit. Declining rather than carrying forward as a "some day" item; reopen if a real program surfaces a need list literals don't cover. **Unrelated, real bug found and fixed while investigating this** (see item 189 above): `f"{n}"`, a bare single-interpolation f-string, silently kept its interpolated value's original type instead of becoming `Text`, causing a runtime segfault — caught while building the `[1,2,3].map(double)` verification program above, whose own `println(f"{...}")` output line hit it first. |

### Validators (spec §16, docs/section-16-validators.md)

_Effectively un-audited before this pass — neither of the two prior audits covered it (§16 lives in a separate file from the main spec doc). The feature has real, substantial codebase footprint (parser, resolve, typeck, HIR, codegen, fmt, LSP all touch it) but is far less complete than that footprint suggests — see item 222 below for the overall assessment._

| # | Task | Notes |
|---|------|-------|
| 224 | `V.validate`/`V.validateAll`'s call-site signature (registered in `crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`'s `hoist_decls`) always has 1 parameter (`entity` only), even when the validator has a `context` block — so the correct, spec-shaped 2-argument call (`V.validate(entity, ctx)`) a context-using validator actually needs is rejected by the type checker | Split out of item 220's own investigation while trying to verify a context-block validator fully end-to-end. The real generated C function (fixed under item 220 to correctly take `(entity, ctx)`) and the type-checker's own idea of the function's signature (`entity` only) disagree. Needs a real design decision, not a guess: should the synthesized `{Name}Context` type be resolved *nominally* (`Ty::Named("{Name}Context")`), which requires `expand_validators` to have already spliced that type's own declaration into the module *before* `hoist_decls` runs — true today only for `certo build`/`run`'s pipeline, never `certo check`'s (a separate, real, undocumented divergence between what `check` and `build`/`run` each actually verify — noted, not fixed, under item 220); or should the context parameter's type be an anonymous *structural* `Ty::Record` built directly from `v.context`'s own fields (sidestepping the `certo check`-vs-`build` ordering question entirely, at the cost of needing `Ty::Record`-vs-`Ty::Named` structural compatibility to already work cleanly at the call site, which hasn't been confirmed either way). **A second, related, but distinct bug found investigating the same "is a validator's own generated function known outside `emit_validator.rs`'s own codegen text?" question**: `V.validate(entity).isOk()` (UFCS dot-call on a validator's own `Result`) fails to compile (`member reference base type 'int64_t' is not a structure or union`) even for the simplest possible context-*free* validator, while `match r { Ok(_) => ..., Err(_) => ... }` on the exact same value correctly sees the real `Result<Unit, ErrorsType>` — `crates/hir/src/lower.rs`'s `lower_module` pre-registration pass (which builds the call-return-type table UFCS dot-call resolution depends on) only walks `Decl::Fn`/`Decl::Impl`, never `Decl::Validator`. Both stem from the same root cause: nothing outside `emit_validator.rs`'s own codegen text output has a structured, queryable idea of a validator's generated functions' real signatures. |
| 225 | `E0704` (constraint field not in scope) needs a real deferred-checking mechanism, and `W0100`/`W0101`/`W0102` need a warning-diagnostic subsystem that doesn't exist anywhere in this codebase | Split out of item 220 (see Completed table above) at the user's own explicit direction, after item 220's investigation showed both were much bigger than "implement 3 more diagnostics." **E0704**: today a constraint's body is never checked against anything — `Decl::Constraint` hoists its name as a bare global `Ty::Bool` (`crates/typeck/src/infer_decl.rs`) with zero body inference, anywhere, ever (confirmed: a constraint referencing a completely undefined field never errors). Item 220's own fix inlines a constraint's body into the *generated* validator source (so it's checked *once expanded*, indirectly, as part of the spliced function) — but that only runs under `certo build`/`run` (`expand_validators` isn't called by `certo check`, see item 224), and even when it does run, a scope violation would surface as a generic `E0206 undefined name`, not the spec's own dedicated, more actionable `E0704` naming the constraint and the missing context field. A real E0704 needs the *direct*, non-expanded `Decl::Validator` check (`infer_decl.rs`'s own `check_decl` arm, which runs under `certo check` too) to inline-check each rule's constraint references against that validator's own `context`/entity scope directly — real, new, deferred-resolution logic, not a small addition. **W0100/W0101/W0102**: this codebase has no warning-diagnostic class anywhere today (grepped `crates/cli`/`crates/typeck`/`crates/resolve` for any `W0`/non-fatal-diagnostic infrastructure — zero hits); every diagnostic that exists is a hard compile error. Building these three needs the warning class to exist first (a real, once-only piece of new infrastructure — likely: a parallel `Vec<Warning>` alongside `Vec<TypeError>`, a CLI rendering path that prints but doesn't abort, and a `--strict`-style promotion path, mirroring how `certo lint`'s own warnings already work but wired into the main typeck pass instead of a separate pass) before the three checks themselves (which are each individually small once that exists: W0100 — two rules with an `overrides` relationship and no distinguishing `priority`; W0101 — an `overrides` rule whose condition is a compile-time-obvious tautology; W0102 — not every context field has `loaded by`). |
| 226 | `loaded by db.<table>.find(...)` — the exact syntax every one of spec section 16's own `context` block examples uses — doesn't type-check anywhere in this codebase; `db` is never a bound name at all | Found while implementing item 220's E0705 check and confirmed via a direct, minimal, non-validator repro (`val x = db.customers.find(5)` produces `E0206: undefined name 'db'` plus cascading `E0205`s). Distinct from the *working* DB query-builder API (`Query.from("...")`.where(...)`, items 68-72b, and the inline-consumption bug item 207 already covers) — `db.<table>.<method>(...)` is a different, implicit per-table-accessor spelling that appears to have zero backing implementation anywhere (grepped `crates/typeck`, `crates/hir`, `crates/stdlib` for any special-case handling of a `db` global or per-table accessor — none found). This blocks E0705, W0102, and the already-generated `validateWithDb`/trigger-context-loading code paths (item 220, item 221) from ever being exercised end-to-end by any validator written exactly as section 16 documents it — every real example needs this syntax to work at all. Needs its own scoping pass to decide whether `db.<table>` should desugar to the existing `Query.from("<Table>")` machinery (likely the smallest real fix, if table-name inference from the accessor name mirrors `certo db pull`'s existing snake_case↔PascalCase convention) or is meant to be a genuinely separate mechanism — not guessed at here. |
| 221 | `validator ... trigger on ...`'s SQL is built internally but silently discarded — never reaches `certo db migrate` | `emit_validator()` does build trigger SQL, but `ValidatorOutput::to_source()` (`emit_validator.rs:25-39`) never includes it, and `crates/cli/src/main.rs:727`'s `expand_validators` only calls `.to_source()` — confirmed via grep of `crates/migrate` and the CLI's db-migrate path that this SQL never surfaces anywhere. A separate, unrelated tool (`certo generate validators --emit-sql`, YAML-driven) does emit real trigger SQL, but that's a different workflow, not what `section-16-validators.md` describes. Moderate fix. |
| 222 | `ruleTest`/`validatorTest` (§16.12) parse (lexer/parser/AST/fmt all handle them) but have zero resolve/typeck/HIR/codegen/testrunner support, and `certo doc` has no validator-awareness at all | Reproduced live: a file with a `ruleTest` block passes `certo check` silently, and `certo test` on it reports "no tests found". `crates/fmt/src/fmt_decl.rs:294,298` formats both block kinds as a literal `{ ... }` placeholder — destroying the body on format rather than round-tripping it. Design-sketch scale (needs real entity/context construction, rule invocation against synthetic data, expectation-checking wired into `certo-testrunner`). **Overall assessment for §16**: the feature parses and has a plausible-looking source-to-source expansion architecture, but none of the "core promise" guarantees stated at the top of its own spec doc (structural soundness, acyclic dependency verification, typed-error verification) are actually enforced, the admin-override pattern that's the headline use case is silently broken (item 218), and testing/doc/trigger integrations are all inert. Likely needs its own design-sketch/scoping pass rather than being patched piecemeal — item 217 (now resolved, see Completed table above) was a hard prerequisite for any of items 218-222 to even be testable; that blocker is now cleared. |
| 223 | A type error inside validator- or state-machine-*generated* code renders against the wrong source text — a real, garbled-output bug, split out of item 217's own investigation | Found while scoping item 217's "related" span bug: `crates/cli/src/main.rs`'s `expand_validators`/`expand_state_machines` each parse their own generated Certo source from a *synthetic* string (codegen reprints the user's `require`/`else` expressions to text, then re-parses that text fresh — the original expression nodes/spans are never reused), then merge the resulting declarations into the real module with no origin tracking at all. `certo_ast::span::Span` is a bare `{start: u32, end: u32}` with no source/file identifier anywhere in the codebase, so a spliced declaration's spans can (and typically do) numerically collide with the original file's own span range. `run_typeck_inner` (`crates/cli/src/main.rs`) always renders every diagnostic against the one original file's `src`/`filename`, so any type error whose span falls inside spliced code shows wrong, unrelated source text. Confirmed no existing "rewrite every span in a subtree" visitor exists anywhere in the codebase to build on — a real fix needs one, at minimum scoped to the `Fn`/`Type` declaration shapes `emit_validator.rs`/`emit_state_machine.rs` actually produce (not the full AST grammar), to either offset spliced spans into a non-colliding range or tag them with an origin so rendering can pick the right source string — mirroring the pattern `expand_validators`/`expand_state_machines` already use for their own *parse*-failure case (`render_all(&diags, &wrapped, "<generated validator>", colour)`), which only ever fires when the generated text itself fails to parse, not for a later type error. Medium-bounded once scoped to just those decl/expr shapes, not a small patch — real new infrastructure, not attempted here. |

_Audit method (2026-07-16): three parallel code-vs-spec sweeps across all crates, cross-checked with `docs/LIMITATIONS.md` and direct grep/read of the relevant parser/typeck/codegen/stdlib source. Items above are things confirmed absent or stubbed in code, not just absent from this file._

_Audit method (2026-08-13, items 143-166): four parallel code-vs-spec sweeps, one per group of spec sections (§§2-5, §§6-8, §§9-10, §§11-13), each cross-checked against BACKLOG.md's Completed and Pending sections first to avoid re-flagging known gaps, then verified against the real parser/typeck/hir/mir/codegen/stdlib source and, wherever practical, a real compiled `certo.exe` run against a minimal repro `.cto` file (not just read-and-guess) — every item above is annotated with its own confirmation method. This second pass was run after items 76/108-142 landed, specifically to catch drift introduced or exposed by that work as well as gaps the first pass missed._

_Audit method (2026-08-20, items 195-222): four parallel code-vs-spec sweeps, one per group (§§2-5, §§6-8, §§9-10, §§11-13 plus the separately-filed §16 Validators), each cross-checked against BACKLOG.md's Completed and Pending sections first to avoid re-flagging known gaps, then verified against the real parser/resolve/typeck/hir/mir/codegen/stdlib/ui source and, wherever practical, a real compiled `certo.exe`/`certo-ui.exe` run against a minimal repro `.cto` file, including several against a live local PostgreSQL instance — not just read-and-guess. This third pass was run after items 76/108-194 and the current session's items 178/166/88 landed, specifically to catch drift introduced by that work as well as gaps the first two passes missed; §16 Validators in particular had received no prior audit coverage at all._

## Critical path to a running program

```
AST → Parser → Resolve → Typeck → HIR → MIR → Codegen (C) → clang → binary
 1       2        3         4       8     8         9
```

The core pipeline (parse → HIR → MIR → C → binary) is fully operational.
Stdlib names are pre-seeded into the type environment; `Ok`/`Err` are built-in.
`certo build` reads `entry` and `output` from `certo.toml`; `certo db` subcommands are wired.
